I do construction, so I can’t really be dragging like that. I mean, I’ve dealt with rough places before, but this one’s just… it’s got me all sideways.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things that you usually like doing, like hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really thought about it like that. I mean, I haven’t been doing much besides dealing with that room and getting to work, so it’s hard to say.\n\nLike, I used to go see people or mess around with stuff after work, but lately it’s more like I get home and there’s some stupid thing wrong again, and then I’m just stuck on that. So I don’t know if I’m not enjoying stuff, or if I’m just not really getting to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested or less motivated to do things you normally enjoy, even when you’ve had the chance?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. Like, if I get a free minute, it’s not like I’m thinking, “Oh, I don’t want to do that anymore.” It’s more like my head goes to, okay, did I leave that thing sitting out, or is something else gonna be messed up when I get back.\n\nI mean, I used to like just hanging out, going over to my buddy’s place, watching a game or whatever. But then, you know, his brother’s always got people coming through, and last time I was over there somebody was talking about needing a guy for a side job, and then I’m thinking about work again, and— sorry, I don’t know. It’s just hard to tell right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down or depressed most of the day, even when there’s no immediate problem with the room or work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it depressed, like all day depressed. Like I’ll be sitting there and then remember this one place I stayed at years ago where the sink backed up for like a week, and the landlord kept saying he’d come by, and— sorry. I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed being sad all day for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about things, even when you know they might not happen or when there’s not much you can do about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I guess that’s kind of why I came in. It’s not like I want to be worrying about it, but it just keeps taking over.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like restlessness, feeling on edge, trouble sleeping, or muscle tension since all this started with the room?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of wound up. I’ll mess up things I normally handle fine, and then that just gets me more worked up. It’s like the room follows me around, even when I’m not there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying about other things besides the room and work, like your health, relationships, or things that used to not bother you much?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really? Like one thing leads to the other. I guess other little things bother me more lately, but I don’t know if that counts. It’s not like I suddenly started worrying about everything. It just keeps circling back to that place.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control or stop worrying about these things, even when you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I try to stop, but it doesn’t really shut off. I’ll tell myself, just leave it, go to bed, but then I’m back up checking the leak or messing with the heat or whatever stupid thing broke in that room.\n\nAnd it’s like, I came in because it’s not just annoying anymore. I’m snapping at people, then I’m dragging myself late to work the next day and messing up stuff I normally handle fine. I used to be able to just get through the day, you know? Now it keeps looping back around, even when I’m trying not to think about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not sure. I mean, bills and stuff are always there, you know? Everybody’s got that. I don’t think I’m suddenly worried about every little thing.

Like my buddy was telling me the other day his truck needs a transmission, and then I started thinking about my own truck because it’s been making this weird noise—but that’s normal, right? When you work construction, if your truck goes down, you’re kind of screwed. But I don’t sit there all day worrying about groceries or people calling me back or whatever.

It mostly still comes back to the room and what I’ve got to deal with because of it. Other stuff maybe bugs me quicker than before, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying about a bunch of different things.

Yeah… work, definitely. I don’t really think about friends judging me or whatever, not much. But at work, yeah, because now it feels like people are watching if I’m slipping.

I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. I mean, construction wears you out anyway, you know? Some days I come home and I’m beat, but that’s kind of normal if you’ve been hauling stuff around or up early.

Ever since I moved into that busted-up room, it’s like my head won’t leave it alone. I’ll be at work thinking, is the heat gonna quit again, is that leak gonna get worse, did I put something under it, and then I get mad over dumb stuff.\n\nAnd then I’m up late messing with it, trying to fix whatever’s wrong, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late and screwing up things I usually handle fine. It’s not like I want to be worrying about it, but it just keeps taking over.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like restlessness, feeling on edge, trouble sleeping, or muscle tension since all this started with the room?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of wound up. Like I’ll be trying to sleep, but then I hear something or think about the heat going out again, or that leak, and I’m up messing with it instead of just leaving it alone.\n\nThen the next day I’m dragging into work late, and I’m snapping at people over little stuff, which isn’t really how I try to be. I’ll mess up things I normally handle fine, and then that just gets me more worked up. It’s like the room follows me around, even when I’m not there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying about other things besides the room and work, like your health, relationships, or things that used to not bother you much?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really? I mean, I’m not sitting around thinking I’m sick or that everybody hates me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s mostly the room stuff, and then work because the room stuff messes with work. Like one thing leads to the other. I guess other little things bother me more lately, but I don’t know if that counts. It’s not like I suddenly started worrying about everything. It just keeps circling back to that place.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control or stop worrying about these things, even when you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I try to stop, but it doesn’t really shut off. I’ll tell myself, just leave it, go to bed, but then I’m back up checking the leak or messing with the heat or whatever stupid thing broke in that room.\n\nAnd it’s like, I came in because it’s not just annoying anymore. I’m snapping at people, then I’m dragging myself late to work the next day and messing up stuff I normally handle fine. I used to be able to just get through the day, you know? Now it keeps looping back around, even when I’m trying not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s nothing specific going on, like when you’re at rest or in moments where there’s no immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess not like out of nowhere exactly. It’s more like even when I’m sitting still, nothing’s happening right then, my head still goes back to that room. Like, is the heat gonna cut out again, is that leak gonna be worse, do I need to deal with it before morning.\n\nAnd then I’m up late messing with stuff, and next day I’m dragging into work late, screwing up things I usually handle fine. Then I’m snapping at people, and I hate that, because that’s not really me. So yeah, even when there’s not some big thing happening that second, it’s still kind of sitting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a number of different things, not just the room or work, like other responsibilities or everyday events, more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, bills and stuff are always there, you know? Everybody’s got that. I don’t think I’m suddenly worried about every little thing.\n\nLike my buddy was telling me the other day his truck needs a transmission, and then I started thinking about my own truck because it’s been making this weird noise—but that’s normal, right? When you work construction, if your truck goes down, you’re kind of screwed. But I don’t sit there all day worrying about groceries or people calling me back or whatever.\n\nIt mostly still comes back to the room and what I’ve got to deal with because of it. Other stuff maybe bugs me quicker than before, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying about a bunch of different things.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling anxious or worried about events or activities that you wouldn’t have usually stressed about before, even when they’re not directly related to the room or work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I mean, little stuff gets on my nerves more, but I can’t tell if I’m actually worried about it or just already wound up.\n\nLike, I had to go to the hardware store and the guy there was taking forever finding something, and I was standing there thinking, “come on, man,” but that’s not really anxiety, right? That’s just… people being slow. Sorry, I’m probably not answering it right.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself stressing about random stuff that never used to bother me. It mostly circles back to the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself unusually anxious or worried about how you’re doing at work or in situations where others might judge your performance, like when you're with friends or in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… work, definitely. I don’t really think about friends judging me or whatever, not much. But at work, yeah, because now it feels like people are watching if I’m slipping.\n\nMy foreman already pulled me aside and said if I keep messing up, I could lose my chance at moving toward crew lead. And I mean, I was kind of hoping for that, you know? It’s not like I’m trying to be some big boss, but it’d be steady, better money maybe.\n\nAnd then there’s been dumb paperwork stuff and scheduling stuff I’ve screwed up because of all this, and that makes me look worse. So yeah, I guess I do get anxious about how I’m doing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a loss of energy nearly every day, even when you haven't been dealing with the room or work stress?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. I mean, construction wears you out anyway, you know? Some days I come home and I’m beat, but that’s kind of normal if you’ve been hauling stuff around or up early.\n\nLike yesterday this guy on the crew was saying he’s tired all the time too, but he’s got two kids and barely sleeps, so… I don’t know, everybody’s tired. For me, I notice it more when all this stuff is going on in my head, but if you’re asking if I’m just low energy all the time even when nothing’s happening, I’m not really sure I’ve noticed that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t think it’s, like, everything in my life, no. I mean, bills are annoying and errands are annoying, but I don’t sit around worrying about all of it the way some people do.

It’s more like once something reminds me of the room or work, then my head goes there. Like I’ll be doing something totally normal, getting gas or whatever, and then I’ll think about having to be at work the next day, or something I forgot, and then I’m stuck on that for a while.

But other responsibilities? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed myself worrying about a bunch of different stuff all day. Mostly it comes back to the same things.

Now it keeps looping back around, even when I’m trying not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s nothing specific going on, like when you’re at rest or in moments where there’s no immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess not like out of nowhere exactly. I’m not sure. I don’t think it’s a whole variety of stuff, if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sitting there worried about every little thing.

Now it keeps looping back around, even when I’m trying not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s nothing specific going on, like when you’re at rest or in moments where there’s no immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess not like out of nowhere exactly. I don’t know about six months. That sounds like a long time when you say it like that. I mean, I worry, yeah, but “many different things” makes it sound like I’m worried about, like, the weather and my truck and some random thing on the news all day.

Ever since I moved into that busted-up room, it’s like my head won’t leave it alone. I’ll be at work thinking, is the heat gonna quit again, is that leak gonna get worse, did I put something under it, and then I get mad over dumb stuff.\n\nAnd then I’m up late messing with it, trying to fix whatever’s wrong, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late and screwing up things I usually handle fine. It’s not like I want to be worrying about it, but it just keeps taking over.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like restlessness, feeling on edge, trouble sleeping, or muscle tension since all this started with the room?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of wound up. Like I’ll be trying to sleep, but then I hear something or think about the heat going out again, or that leak, and I’m up messing with it instead of just leaving it alone.\n\nThen the next day I’m dragging into work late, and I’m snapping at people over little stuff, which isn’t really how I try to be. I’ll mess up things I normally handle fine, and then that just gets me more worked up. It’s like the room follows me around, even when I’m not there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying about other things besides the room and work, like your health, relationships, or things that used to not bother you much?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really? I mean, I’m not sitting around thinking I’m sick or that everybody hates me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s mostly the room stuff, and then work because the room stuff messes with work. Like one thing leads to the other. I guess other little things bother me more lately, but I don’t know if that counts. It’s not like I suddenly started worrying about everything. It just keeps circling back to that place.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control or stop worrying about these things, even when you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I try to stop, but it doesn’t really shut off. I’ll tell myself, just leave it, go to bed, but then I’m back up checking the leak or messing with the heat or whatever stupid thing broke in that room.\n\nAnd it’s like, I came in because it’s not just annoying anymore. I’m snapping at people, then I’m dragging myself late to work the next day and messing up stuff I normally handle fine. I used to be able to just get through the day, you know? Now it keeps looping back around, even when I’m trying not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s nothing specific going on, like when you’re at rest or in moments where there’s no immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess not like out of nowhere exactly. It’s more like even when I’m sitting still, nothing’s happening right then, my head still goes back to that room. Like, is the heat gonna cut out again, is that leak gonna be worse, do I need to deal with it before morning.\n\nAnd then I’m up late messing with stuff, and next day I’m dragging into work late, screwing up things I usually handle fine. Then I’m snapping at people, and I hate that, because that’s not really me. So yeah, even when there’s not some big thing happening that second, it’s still kind of sitting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a number of different things, not just the room or work, like other responsibilities or everyday events, more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, bills and stuff are always there, you know? Everybody’s got that. I don’t think I’m suddenly worried about every little thing.\n\nLike my buddy was telling me the other day his truck needs a transmission, and then I started thinking about my own truck because it’s been making this weird noise—but that’s normal, right? When you work construction, if your truck goes down, you’re kind of screwed. But I don’t sit there all day worrying about groceries or people calling me back or whatever.\n\nIt mostly still comes back to the room and what I’ve got to deal with because of it. Other stuff maybe bugs me quicker than before, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying about a bunch of different things.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling anxious or worried about events or activities that you wouldn’t have usually stressed about before, even when they’re not directly related to the room or work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I mean, little stuff gets on my nerves more, but I can’t tell if I’m actually worried about it or just already wound up.\n\nLike, I had to go to the hardware store and the guy there was taking forever finding something, and I was standing there thinking, “come on, man,” but that’s not really anxiety, right? That’s just… people being slow. Sorry, I’m probably not answering it right.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself stressing about random stuff that never used to bother me. It mostly circles back to the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself unusually anxious or worried about how you’re doing at work or in situations where others might judge your performance, like when you're with friends or in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… work, definitely. I don’t really think about friends judging me or whatever, not much. But at work, yeah, because now it feels like people are watching if I’m slipping.\n\nMy foreman already pulled me aside and said if I keep messing up, I could lose my chance at moving toward crew lead. And I mean, I was kind of hoping for that, you know? It’s not like I’m trying to be some big boss, but it’d be steady, better money maybe.\n\nAnd then there’s been dumb paperwork stuff and scheduling stuff I’ve screwed up because of all this, and that makes me look worse. So yeah, I guess I do get anxious about how I’m doing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a loss of energy nearly every day, even when you haven't been dealing with the room or work stress?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. I mean, construction wears you out anyway, you know? Some days I come home and I’m beat, but that’s kind of normal if you’ve been hauling stuff around or up early.\n\nLike yesterday this guy on the crew was saying he’s tired all the time too, but he’s got two kids and barely sleeps, so… I don’t know, everybody’s tired. For me, I notice it more when all this stuff is going on in my head, but if you’re asking if I’m just low energy all the time even when nothing’s happening, I’m not really sure I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about more than one thing in your life, like not just the room or work but maybe other responsibilities or parts of your day, even when you’re not right in the middle of dealing with them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s, like, everything in my life, no. I mean, bills are annoying and errands are annoying, but I don’t sit around worrying about all of it the way some people do.\n\nIt’s more like once something reminds me of the room or work, then my head goes there. Like I’ll be doing something totally normal, getting gas or whatever, and then I’ll think about having to be at work the next day, or something I forgot, and then I’m stuck on that for a while.\n\nBut other responsibilities? I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed myself worrying about a bunch of different stuff all day. Mostly it comes back to the same things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a variety of different things in your life — not just the room or work — even when those things aren’t happening or aren’t that big a deal?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think it’s a whole variety of stuff, if that’s what you mean. Like, I’m not sitting there worried about every little thing.\n\nSometimes my mind wanders, though. Like I’ll start thinking about one thing and then it turns into something else, but usually it kind of circles back to the same stuff. I mean, life stuff comes up, sure, but I don’t know that I’d call it worrying about a bunch of different things for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying about many different things in your life — even small or unlikely things — on most days for at least six months?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about six months. That sounds like a long time when you say it like that. I mean, I worry, yeah, but “many different things” makes it sound like I’m worried about, like, the weather and my truck and some random thing on the news all day.\n\nIt’s more like… certain stuff gets in my head and then it sticks there. Like I’ll be at the store and forget why I came in, and then I’m thinking about work or whatever, but then I’ll see someone buying those energy drinks and remember this guy I worked with who used to drink like four of them before lunch—anyway, no, I don’t think it’s been most days for six months about all kinds of little things. I haven’t really kept track like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean… I don’t know. Not really a whole bunch of different things, I don’t think. It’s hard to separate it out, because once I’m already worked up about the room or work, then other stuff can kind of jump in my head, you know?

I guess I haven’t noticed myself worrying about a bunch of random things when nothing’s going on. It mostly feels tied to the same couple things.

I mean, I’ve dealt with rough places before, but this one’s just… it’s got me all sideways.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things that you usually like doing, like hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really thought about it like that. So I don’t know if I’m not enjoying stuff, or if I’m just not really getting to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested or less motivated to do things you normally enjoy, even when you’ve had the chance?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. It’s not like I want to be worrying about it, but it just keeps taking over.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like restlessness, feeling on edge, trouble sleeping, or muscle tension since all this started with the room?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of wound up. Like I’ll be trying to sleep, but then I hear something or think about the heat going out again, or that leak, and I’m up messing with it instead of just leaving it alone.\n\nThen the next day I’m dragging into work late, and I’m snapping at people over little stuff, which isn’t really how I try to be. I’ll mess up things I normally handle fine, and then that just gets me more worked up. It’s like the room follows me around, even when I’m not there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying about other things besides the room and work, like your health, relationships, or things that used to not bother you much?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really? Like one thing leads to the other. I guess other little things bother me more lately, but I don’t know if that counts. It’s not like I suddenly started worrying about everything. It just keeps circling back to that place.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control or stop worrying about these things, even when you try not to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I try to stop, but it doesn’t really shut off. I’ll tell myself, just leave it, go to bed, but then I’m back up checking the leak or messing with the heat or whatever stupid thing broke in that room.\n\nAnd it’s like, I came in because it’s not just annoying anymore. I’m snapping at people, then I’m dragging myself late to work the next day and messing up stuff I normally handle fine. I used to be able to just get through the day, you know? Now it keeps looping back around, even when I’m trying not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried even when there’s nothing specific going on, like when you’re at rest or in moments where there’s no immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess not like out of nowhere exactly. It’s more like even when I’m sitting still, nothing’s happening right then, my head still goes back to that room. Like, is the heat gonna cut out again, is that leak gonna be worse, do I need to deal with it before morning.\n\nAnd then I’m up late messing with stuff, and next day I’m dragging into work late, screwing up things I usually handle fine. Then I’m snapping at people, and I hate that, because that’s not really me. So yeah, even when there’s not some big thing happening that second, it’s still kind of sitting on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a number of different things, not just the room or work, like other responsibilities or everyday events, more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, bills and stuff are always there, you know? Everybody’s got that. I don’t think I’m suddenly worried about every little thing.\n\nLike my buddy was telling me the other day his truck needs a transmission, and then I started thinking about my own truck because it’s been making this weird noise—but that’s normal, right? When you work construction, if your truck goes down, you’re kind of screwed. But I don’t sit there all day worrying about groceries or people calling me back or whatever.\n\nIt mostly still comes back to the room and what I’ve got to deal with because of it. Other stuff maybe bugs me quicker than before, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying about a bunch of different things.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling anxious or worried about events or activities that you wouldn’t have usually stressed about before, even when they’re not directly related to the room or work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really, I guess. I mean, little stuff gets on my nerves more, but I can’t tell if I’m actually worried about it or just already wound up.\n\nLike, I had to go to the hardware store and the guy there was taking forever finding something, and I was standing there thinking, “come on, man,” but that’s not really anxiety, right? That’s just… people being slow. Sorry, I’m probably not answering it right.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself stressing about random stuff that never used to bother me. It mostly circles back to the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself unusually anxious or worried about how you’re doing at work or in situations where others might judge your performance, like when you're with friends or in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… work, definitely. I don’t really think about friends judging me or whatever, not much. But at work, yeah, because now it feels like people are watching if I’m slipping.\n\nMy foreman already pulled me aside and said if I keep messing up, I could lose my chance at moving toward crew lead. And I mean, I was kind of hoping for that, you know? It’s not like I’m trying to be some big boss, but it’d be steady, better money maybe.\n\nAnd then there’s been dumb paperwork stuff and scheduling stuff I’ve screwed up because of all this, and that makes me look worse. So yeah, I guess I do get anxious about how I’m doing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a loss of energy nearly every day, even when you haven't been dealing with the room or work stress?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. I mean, construction wears you out anyway, you know? Some days I come home and I’m beat, but that’s kind of normal if you’ve been hauling stuff around or up early.\n\nLike yesterday this guy on the crew was saying he’s tired all the time too, but he’s got two kids and barely sleeps, so… I don’t know, everybody’s tired. For me, I notice it more when all this stuff is going on in my head, but if you’re asking if I’m just low energy all the time even when nothing’s happening, I’m not really sure I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about more than one thing in your life, like not just the room or work but maybe other responsibilities or parts of your day, even when you’re not right in the middle of dealing with them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s, like, everything in my life, no. I mean, bills are annoying and errands are annoying, but I don’t sit around worrying about all of it the way some people do.\n\nIt’s more like once something reminds me of the room or work, then my head goes there. I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed myself worrying about a bunch of different stuff all day. Mostly it comes back to the same things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a variety of different things in your life — not just the room or work — even when those things aren’t happening or aren’t that big a deal?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I haven’t really kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling anxious or worried on most days for at least six months?\n\nPatient: No, not six months. I guess I haven’t noticed myself worrying about a bunch of random things when nothing’s going on. It mostly feels tied to the same couple things."
